ParlaMint-AT_2012-12-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00184_d8e9606	Ladies and gentlemen! Mr Ikrath spoke of a big breakthrough, I would appreciate it differently: It is a law of granted opportunities. We wanted a fundamental break in the re-regulation of care. We have formulated a goal, namely that the dispute of care and the dispute of the case-law of the Court of First Instance come to conciliation points. In the context of whether or not there is a right to visit, legal disputes must only be resolved in the second place, primarily in relation to something quite different, namely, to resolve separation conflicts. And one thing is clear: For this, dishes are the wrong place. Not because our judges are poorly qualified, no – I am a lawyer myself, I am a strong lawyer and I know that we have good judges – but because we need other instruments and methods in relation conflicts – and these are separation conflicts. We need a conciliation body that makes it possible for common solutions to be developed, because the best solutions are not always those that decree the law, or most often not those that decree the law, but the best solutions that one develops together. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] This opportunity would have been offered by the Conciliation Body, and it was not used. I am convinced that 90 percent of all separation conflicts related to care and visitation rights could be resolved in such conciliation bodies. We have examples from Germany that show this. Experts have also confirmed this. 90 percent! This would have the advantage that those 10 percent who cannot be solved could then go to court and there a clear decision could be made who gets the care, father or mother. Ladies and gentlemen! I once presented the proposal with the conciliation bodies to a journalist, and at the end of the day the journalist, after listening to it, said: Who can have anything against such an idea at all? I said I don't know, you ask the wrong person, I'm convinced. But I can confirm that in all the conversations I have conducted, that was seen positively. The vast number of experts have given me the right thing, even the judges themselves have said, yes, it is true that it is difficult for us to make decisions on whether or not there is a right to visit, it actually needs other professionals to do so. Mothers, fathers – no matter who I have discussed with – all have said yes, we need such instruments so that we can work out common solutions. ParlaMint-AT_2012-12-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00184_d8e10048	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! Values Advisors at the visitor gallery and in front of the TV screens! When I took up the office of the Minister of Justice more than a year and a half ago, it was clear to me that in addition to a wealth of other tasks and challenges, I had to deal with two topics in particular. On the one hand, this is to strengthen confidence in justice; it is about combating corruption and economic crime. And, of course, it's a matter of course – that was a special concern to me – to create a modern, new family law that is adapted to today's social structures. In my opinion, we owe this new, modern family law to the families in Austria, but above all to the children, because especially the children are far too often the sufferers if the parents can no longer agree. That is why it was important to me from the start to present a comprehensive legislative package – and not just as you mentioned it, Mrs Haubner, a mere repair law. This package of legislation goes beyond a mere repair at Weitem. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] It is not only the necessary implementation of the Constitutional Court's knowledge and knowledge of the European Court of Human Rights, but we see many further innovations in this package. From a new regulation of the care and the right to visit, to the acceleration of the procedure, to a modern family-friendly name right, all this is included in this package. Of course, this package also includes, in implementation of the highest judicial findings, a new right of application for fathers of immoral children. Negotiations started about three years ago and were now able to reach an agreement which, in my view, is a very good solution. I am very glad that, in constructive negotiations with Mr Gabriele Heinisch-Hosekgelungen, it is really a breakthrough here, a breakthrough for a modern family law that focuses only on child welfare. And I am now at a very important point, ladies and gentlemen: This family law is not about a mother's law, it's not about a father's law, it's about a law that focuses on the interests of children who focus on the child's well-being. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Because – I would like to stress it again – it is the children who are suffering very much from separation. That is why it is also particularly important – and I am pleased that this has also been emphasized by all the Members who have spoken so far – that the child's well-being is defined in detail. The new family law contains a new, detailed definition of the child's well-being and thus provides a much better and more fundamental basis for decision-making for family decisions and is therefore really groundbreaking. I have also always stressed that I pursue three main objectives with the Family Law Package, and these are also achieved by the present draft. On the one hand, and this has already been mentioned several times in order to speed up the procedures. There must no longer be any alienation due to the duration of the procedures. Secondly, I am concerned with maintaining as far as possible continuity for children. As I said, they are suffering from separation, they are suffering disputes between parents, but they want to have both parents, and as far as possible, in line with the child's welfare, there is a need to preserve continuity for children, both as regards care and the right of visitation. Finally, of course, it is also a question of a decent solution, of creating a human rights-compliant solution, and here, too, the recognition of the European Court of Human Rights, the realization of the Constitutional Court, that is to say the right to apply for sole or common care to fathers. Ladies and gentlemen! One of my main points, in my opinion, in this new law, is that in the future the common care will become a regular case, because it will usually be the best for the child when father and mother are there for her when both parents are there for the child. After a period of provisional parental responsibility, family judges will in future be able to agree not only to the sole care, but also to the common care of both parents, even in the event of disputed divorces. And this preliminary phase of parental responsibility is not a probationary phase, as it is often meant. Both Mr Fichtenbauer and Mrs Haubner have spoken about probation. Mrs Haubner spoke of the need for fathers to ‘prove’. I would like to point out that it is a question of both parents exercising rights and obligations that both parents are responsible for the child. And this is what this phase of provisional parental responsibility is all about. Faster procedures – I would like to address this very central issue once again, because it was always a key issue when I was addressed by affected fathers and mothers. I have heard over and over again that the procedures take too long to be insidiously burdened on parents, but especially on children. That's why we have to sit here. And through the new family law, but above all through an increase in the area of family judges, the procedures will be accelerated. Mrs Haubner asked whether we really have sufficient personnel at our disposal. The answer is: Yes. I am very glad that the budget negotiations have succeeded in obtaining 93 additional posts for the field of justice, and a large part of these 93 planning areas goes to the family courts to speed up the procedures here. Mr Steinhauser referred to something very essential, namely that it is of course a question of resolving relationship conflicts. Only we have different solutions: They want to do so in a pre-arranged conciliation procedure; we use the family court procedure and there we see new additional possibilities for the reconciliation of family conflicts, of relationship conflicts. In the future, for example, it is possible for judges to arrange the compulsory visit of educational advice, parental advice, or an initial discussion about mediation or a conciliation procedure. All of this is planned to resolve relationship conflicts. And, of course, it's very important that there is also family aid. Family courtship should help parents to resolve the relationship conflicts so that they no longer have looked at the child’s welfare. Family judicial assistance is intended to enable parents to open their eyes to the child’s well-being. And the Family Justice Service manages to reach common solutions in many cases. ParlaMint-AT_2012-12-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00184_d8e11026	Next speaker: Mrs Himmelbauer. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-12-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00184_d8e11047	Mr President. Federal Ministers! Ladies and gentlemen. The fact that relationships do not always end in good is not a novelty. And that the waves can go up and decisions can not always be made rationally is not a novelty either. But if children are in play, it must be clear that personal circumstances must be resettled – for the benefit of the child. This child welfare is now found for the first time in the Childhood and Name Rights Amendment Act, which is to be voted. Twelve criteria listed in the law ensure that the child's well-being is taken into account and guaranteed as a guiding consideration. With these twelve criteria as the highest principle, a family law package has been created that allows for faster decisions in family court proceedings and continuity for children even after the separation of parents. The duration of the proceedings is significantly reduced by the use of the family court aid. The positive experience has also been demonstrated by the model experiment in the four District Courts since the beginning of the year; they also confirm the nationwide expansion. Family conflicts are also to be resolved more quickly and more satisfactorily for all parties. And, of course, that's for the child's good. The care of both parents after a separation remains the same for the time being. The common care should therefore also be the case in the future. The Family Law Package includes, in addition to the right to contact, the care after the separation, the instrument of the visitor, the equality of father and mother, the further, more flexible name right that allows the wearing of a double name – not required, but also possible – and thus also contributes to the creation or increase of family identity. Thus, marital and illegitimate children are equated and the determination of the family name is facilitated without lengthy procedures. All these points of this proposal for a law contribute to strengthening and safeguarding the child's well-being.

I remember, however, that it is this foot shackle ==References== There are rigid restrictions on September 2012: First of all, we must not exceed the twelve-month penalty; we need a suitable home for the offender; he or she must have an employment; he or she must have an income from which one can live; he or she must be ill and accident insurance; and the persons living in the common household must also agree. Yes, I understand for victims, and it's certainly difficult to know that a culprit is going around with a footband. But there are, of course, constitutional concerns. There are not whole groups of perpetrators to be excluded. More stringent criteria are now being proposed, taking into account criticism of the restrictive application of the electronically monitored house arrest: Half of the prison sentence must be fined in the execution centre, but at least three months; there must be a favourable forecast that this offender does not abuse the house arrest; the victim has a right of expression and information if it wants; and additional conditions can also be made, for example, a GPS prison, which significantly restricts the movement of the perpetrator. Now for video surveillance of institutions: There is the possibility to monitor the execution facilities and their external borders by means of video cameras. This can be in line with the legal mandate of the appropriate execution of the sentence. Video surveillance serves to safeguard inmates, it serves to avoid attempts to escape, it also serves to protect public security and prevent crimes. The data protection provisions are taken into account. The head of the institution is authorized to use the video surveillance, but he does not have to do that. Video surveillance thus increases the sense of security of both the inmates and the staff there, for example those who work in the execution facilities as therapists or as social workers. At the same time, it increases the freedom of movement of the inmates and allows savings in personnel. All in all, it is a law that better regulates the electronically monitored house arrest, and the video surveillance is well regulated here. I think it would be good if the opposition could also decide to agree to this law.

The government programme provides for an evaluation of competition law. The present debate and decision-making is the result of this. Two major changes, improvements and further developments are foreseen. On the one hand, in the antitrust law, it is a question of adapting an Austrian bagtelling exception by means of a corresponding counter-exception for hardcore cartels EU-law. But the more important thing for me is that, on the other hand, supervision of dominant companies is strengthened by adopting the concept of common market governance of German legislation, which has proved itself very successful on this point. On the other hand, decisive importance is attached to improvements in the Competition Act. In the Competition Act, the powers of the Federal Competition Authority towards the companies suspected of using antitrust languages are substantially increased. So we give the Federal Competition Authority new, even sharper teeth by significantly strengthening both its powers to obtain information from companies and by facilitating domestic searches by the Federal Competition Authority or by improving their possibilities in the course of house searches. All in all: a clear development of competition law, where in each individual case the judicial committee has the task of examining very carefully whether government documents actually meet the requirements and principles of our legal tradition in each individual case. If that is not the case, then it is the duty and responsibility of the judicial committee to make amendments.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-12-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00184_d8e13306	We're coming to the 11th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mr Magistrate has spoken on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). Stefan. 3 minutes speaking time. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-12-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00184_d8e13333	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! This is about the basic book registration fee today, i.e. a court fee. A fee that was only increased by 10 percent almost two years ago – not adapted to inflation, but simply increased by 10 percent. Now we have just been presented with a government paper, which can only be described as a shield-citizen's strip. It is always about administrative simplification – rightly discussed – and also about relief from citizens. What was the proposal for this government proposal? - That's the opposite. And it has been found that, in the case of basic book registration fees, it is no longer used for the three-fold unit value – a value determined by the Financial Office that can be retrieved at any time – but for the transport value. The transport value is the value to which a property can be sold, which is of course completely unclear, for which there are no lists to define it there are no precise methods, except one makes an estimate by a judicially sworn expert. Then you could speak of a secure value. That is to say, a regulation has been made which leads to the fact that on the one hand, an official has to look at something in court, and that he has to find out how high this transport value is, whether the information of the parties is correct, i.e. a very high administrative burden. We are talking about 170 000 cases a year. On the other hand, there is an increase in the fee for the citizen by 300 to 1,000 percent. You have to imagine that! That was actually the first proposal that was presented. There were then sharp protests from all sides, including from us, and now there is a relief of the whole. The elimination now looks so that in the case of gifts between relatives – which is defined – the threefold unit value will continue to be used and in the event of transfers to strangers in the gift path of the traffic value. That means that it is already an improvement compared to the first proposal, but still remains: We have the increased administrative burden, because there is now no self-calculation of this fee – that is still to be explained – but this has to be looked at by an official. So I have an increased administrative burden. I have an uncertainty, because, in the event of gifts among strangers, no one knows exactly what you have to certify, and it is unclear what comes out of it. There continues to be an increase of 300 to 1,000 per cent in donations among foreigners. That's still the case, that's reality. Now very briefly to self-calculation, to explain this: To date, it has been possible to calculate the court fee in connection with the tax declaration for the acquisition tax of contractors, lawyers and notaries themselves. They then removed the money from the parties and transferred it to the Financial Office. In other words, the court had no effort at all to do so, in all cases it was able to control it in a sample manner afterwards, but the liability lies anyway with the contractor of the contract that he does it properly. This means a real administrative simplification. That's what you've done now. One has abolished a truly functioning system that saves the state massively. On the other hand, I have the increase in the fee for certain parts of the population by 300 to 1 000 percent. That's the result. I wonder if this was actually just a mistake, if you were wrong, if you just weren't creative enough to find a better solution, or if you wanted to do a favor and introduce the wealth tax through the back door, because it hit wealth. Whoever has a property, it meets. But essentially it also hits the middle class or people who have spared it, because the one who speculates – no, it does not meet all, Herbert Scheibner, and therefore not all, because the one who has it inside a capital company, transfers business shares and therefore has no registration fee in the basic book. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Scheibner.]] [[Abg. Scheibner: The little one hits it!]] Precisely, the little one hits it, right, thank you. The small ones meet it, but those who can regulate it, who have just the assets, the property in a capital company that transfer the shares of that capital company. They don't have a registration fee. That means they save themselves what only the little one meets. There is therefore a completely wrong steering effect, and we therefore reject this law.

This would be an insidious administrative burden that would be created, because everyone sends any ad, which must then be dealt with there by administrative law, the official has to check if this is something for the police or not. If the person who wants to make the ad comes to the service, then you can give him the first information and say that's nothing for the police, that's a private offence or anything, or if he calls during the emergency call or somewhere else. Of course, that would be difficult. There would be a flood of written ads and would again create eerie administrative activities. That is why we will not agree to this request because we do not think it useful. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-12-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00184_d8e16870	We now come to points 17 and 18 of the agenda on which the debate is being held under one. There was no oral reporting. We are going into the debate. As First of all, Mr Prince, has been mentioned. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-12-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00184_d8e16903	Madam President. Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! The present amendment to the Weapons Act regulates the powers of the competent authority in securing and destroying war material, which unfortunately still exists in Austria. Numerous bombs from the Second World War are still suspected in Austria's soils; It is estimated that about 15,000 pieces are expected. In about 30 bombs a year are decomposed by the experts in the demining service. This is a joint task of the Ministry of Interior and Defence. Now these tasks are solely for the Ministry of Defence. Although this law does not yet regulate the acquisition of the costs, there is a clear regulation of the powers of the authorities in respect of compensation. As long as a threat to the population cannot be excluded, a place ban can be imposed and persons can also be rejected until the risk is clarified. The present Novelle explosives law is in principle an EU adaptation which provides for an extension of exceptions to the labelling requirement for civil explosives. It is not necessary to identify explosives that cannot be identified because of lack of packaging, and explosives that represent only a small degree of hazard, such as pulverisation cords or inflammation hats. The law only enters into force with 5. April 2013 in force because, on the one hand, some EU countries still need this time and on the other, the advantage is that the relevant businesses also have the necessary time to adapt to the new changed framework conditions. Our Austrian explosives law is only slightly amended. Everything else remains as decided in the Explosive Act 2010. Shooters and explosives may only be produced by reliable persons residing in Austria, a chemical study and a two-year professional practice. This is prohibited by the use of drugs or criminal offences for acts of violence or similar threats. At the time of storage, the new law requires continuous inventory records, which must also be kept for ten years. The novelle thus brings with it a clear clarification, a concentration on the important and essential. However, proven rules in an extremely sensitive area, which serve the safety of all, remain quite clear.

In the case of renewable energy, Austria is already taking a pioneering role, and worldwide demand for energy and for alternatives to nuclear power is increasing. Austria has a share of 30 percent renewable energy in total energy demand and 70 percent of electricity from renewable energy sources at the top of Europe. And the energy turnaround will continue. Here in this House we only confirmed the consistent continuation of the anti-atomic policy in Austria in the last National Council meeting. We have jointly adopted the Eco electricity Act, which will significantly increase the generation of energy from renewable energy sources by 2020. And our goal of winning at least 85 percent of electricity from renewable energy sources by 2020 is on the right track. However, in order to achieve this objective, we also need to intensify the network development and invest in it. Then: Whether it is wind farms, hydropower or solar energy, consumption is becoming more and more spatially separate from production. And we all know that everything speaks for renewable energy, but no one wants the windwheel at his doorstep or the power line in sight. However, in order to benefit from the great potential of renewable energy, we need to create and expand high-performance networks. The power grid must be adapted to the new forms of production. And in hydropower alone, we have a huge potential for expansion. By 2020, the existing storage potential can be expanded by 60 percent without touching sensitive nature parks or natural areas. The whole energy industry can invest billions in Austria over the next few years in order to put the electricity and energy transition on stable and sustainable legs. Ladies and gentlemen! Investments of over €8 billion will be planned over the next ten years alone for the transmission and distribution networks. These investments are a vital contribution to security of supply in Austria. Of course, transmission system operators play a major role in the field of security of supply in Austria. If we decide on a novel today, then because in 2009 producers successfully objected to a new fee for network losses and pump current in the regulations at the Constitutional Court. Therefore, an amount has been outstanding since then, without which the energy grid operators cannot balance. The consequences of non-balance sheetability would be a damage to the whole economy. It was therefore important and correct to avoid a threatening non-balance sheetability with this technical ElWOG-Novelle. Ladies and gentlemen! We are all clear, renewable energies are the future. However, we also need a clear commitment to the development of the network in Austria, which means the consistent continuation of a successful energy policy. In this sense, we are in favour of ensuring security of supply with this expansion of the networks, but of course also ensuring economic growth and jobs. In this respect, we ensure the best quality of care for Austrians.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-12-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00184_d8e18431	I call Mr Katzian. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-12-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00184_d8e18452	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen! Mr Haubner has already pointed out what is the content of what is to be decided today in connection with the amendment of the ElWOG, the Electricity Economic and Organisation Act. It is about linguistic clarifications and the other. supplementing the accounting rules for network operators. These are small things, you might think, but they have far-reaching effects and are a vital guarantee for transmission system operators. Transmission system operators are those companies that organise the trans-regional transport of electrical energy in the high-voltage network, above all the APG employees, the Austrian Power Grid, who do excellent work and actually create the basis for security of supply in Austria. This change of law creates legal certainty in terms of accounting and allows the responsible activities of transmission system operators to continue to be carried out and guaranteed. It has already been pointed out that the construction of eco-electric power plants is a great development, but this great development also leads to the grids facing major challenges because the flow of electricity is difficult to predict. New centres of energy production are created in places where energy is not needed, whereas energy is needed elsewhere. We must therefore do everything to ensure that energy lands where it is actually needed. But the fact is that we are on our way at two different speeds. A wind farm has a construction period of about two to three years, or it takes about two to three years until it works properly – but it often takes ten years and more until larger management projects can be realized. It is therefore also important to say that if we confess to the expansion of renewable energies – and we confess to them – we must also work to ensure that the ecologically produced electricity ends up with its customers. This requires further efforts at both national and European level. We only received the network development plans of the APG and Vorarlberger Transmission Network GmbH approved by the E-Control last week. These are created annually and in reality form the basis for major infrastructure projects over the next ten years. Investments of around €2.5 billion in the transmission network alone will take place here. If one calculates the investments that the distribution system operators are facing, then we will come to the €8 to €10 billion already mentioned. I think this is good for the economic location, which is good for the expansion of the energy infrastructure, but that is also good for employment. Especially in economically uncertain times, where there is a great movement in the economy, it is important to drive forward investment. If we look at these amounts, here goes a little further, and with them jobs are created, security of supply is created. I think that this is a good basis for further progress in this area too.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-12-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00184_d8e19160	Mr President. Federal Minister! And what's the end in this chain? – In some cases, incapacity for work and thus unemployment, because obviously not everyone can go in early retirement. We know the causes of the increase – there are studies that tell us all again and again: Work compression, tight staffing, blurring the border between working time and leisure time, fear of job loss and also lack of support from superiors – with one word: stress that is becoming increasingly felt in some professions. The aim of this current legislative amendment is to strengthen prevention, i.e. prevention, and to create better networking between labour inspectors, safety professionals, occupational physicians, occupational psychologists and, I would like to expressly mention this, also the works councils in the companies. I would also like to break a lance today for selecting works councils in companies. They also have the task of looking at jobs and in particular at working conditions, and there are fewer problems there in these areas where we have works councils. There are projects of occupational health promotion and much more in many establishments, both in larger and in smaller establishments, and if a worker feels that he comes into a situation where he is severely burdened, then one should also take care of it and perhaps in the company, if there is no works council, engage in choosing a works council. So, common recognition and prevention of hazards is, I believe, a key concept in this context. Therefore: We need jobs that do not make us sick, and today's change of law will contribute to this. - Thank you.

The aim of the Sozialrechts-Amending Act 2012 is to replace the temporary invalidity pension. The draft law aims to further restrict access in the medium and long term so as to ensure the financing of statutory pension insurance. The temporary invalidity pension is, as it were, divided into a re-training allowance or a rehabilitation allowance, or it is replaced. The type of support will also depend on whether re-training is now necessary or whether other offers and measures for professional reintegration are appropriate and reasonable. The AMS will be responsible for professional rehabilitation and re-training fees according to this draft law. Health insurance providers will be responsible for medical rehabilitation. The Federal Minister for Social Affairs and Consumer Protection expects considerable savings in this area. I hope that's true, too. It is a bit positive to evaluate in this area. That's what I'm saying. In particular, I welcome uniform standards in assessing the working capacity of disadvantaged people in the ASVG, BSVG, GSVG in this area. And above all, the inclusion of the people from the professional fire brigade into the night heavy labor law is also very positive. So something in this bill is certainly to be seen positively. Whether the desired measures lead to the final goal, we will see. It's still working on it. Rehabilitation and re-training alone will not bring anything, sir. Federal Minister. I have already said this in the Committee, because it is clear that many, especially the older workers, are currently unemployed and have little prospect of a job. We need jobs for older people. As we see, unemployment is rising. There is no question that we all have to work on making it better. It does nothing if we are still half good at it in the international comparison, but still affected by it. Retraining and rehabilitation do not help if you no longer find jobs afterwards. A re-training is in order, the rehabilitation as well, which is essential. But prevention is better, with re-training, a lot can be done, only if there is no prospect of a job afterwards does not help us. One thing I would like to stress is that the changes to the Social Rights Amendment Act will also be reflected in statistics: the re-trainings in the training statistics of the AMS, the rehabilitation in health insurance, and those in retirement in the retirement age. There is going to be a lot of progress here in the field of statistics. It is important that we fight against rising invalidity pensions. It is also welcomed by our side that you are now doing a lot for the caring family. This is now also contained in this amendment. This is something we can do about it, because in this area we have already made proposals in the past, which contribute to the relief of caring families. We find that positive, but all of this is not far enough for us.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-12-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00184_d8e21759	Mr President. Minister! “After a scientific study by the Hamburg industrial physician Dr. Jürgen Tempel, lecturer at the University of Bremen for “Medical Foundations of Health Sciences”, entitled “Lifetime of Firemans – Impact Analysis” from 1998, professional firearmers in Germany have reduced their life expectancy by seven years due to their high labour load and occupational risk. Then, on average, firemen are only 65,4 years old, while other men in Germany have a life expectancy of 72,7 years. It should be assumed that the situation of professional fire brigades in Austria is comparable. A hard working life, characterized by continuous shift service with all negative effects on social ties (family, circle of friends), hard work with often acute danger to body and life (toxic substances, explosions) and high psychological strains (bizar suicides, death of children), is facing a reduced retirement due to the lower life expectancy." Ladies and gentlemen! This text was tabled as a motion for a resolution on 4. It was introduced here by me in December 2003. We wanted to have a professional image for professional firearms, for a small group that is always there for help but has no lobby. From the law they were auxiliary workers and had up to 65. I have to work for a lifetime. It has now taken almost nine years for the day – nine years with different forms of government, with various ministers that I have greatly tormented to finally make something for the professional fire brigades, whether it was Mr Bartenstein as Minister of Labour at the time, whether it was Mr Mitterlehner or now Mr. Hundstorfer – but it was my concern to finally achieve something for these professional fire brigades. I can only say thank you, Minister Hundstorfer, that we have now managed to open the Night Heavy Labour Act for the professional fire brigades, for those people who really are here for all of us. [[Abg. Dr. Bartenstein: Politics is drilling hard boards!]] With this we can finally give these people back help, we can really allow them to retire well-deserved at the age of 57. Thank you very much, Minister. I can tell you, on behalf of Austrian professional firearms, that they are very excited about this regulation and thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-12-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00184_d8e22033	Next speaker: Mrs Fürntrath-Moretti. 3 minutes speaking time. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-12-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00184_d8e22055	Mr President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen! With this Pension Fund Transfer Act, we take another step towards harmonising pension law in Austria. So far, civil engineers, i.e. architects and engineering consultants, have been insured in an in-house pension insurance system. This would not have been portable in the long term. There was no federal grant to the Civil Engineering Fund. In addition, construction and engineering offices had different pension insurances, resulting in competitive disadvantages. Until now acquired expectations of compulsory periods of practice had been lost. We solve this inequality as well as existing problems with this law. What does that mean? – From 2013, civil technicians will now be transferred to the pension system of self-employed persons. The existing assets of the Pension Fund of the Federal Chamber of Architects and Engineering Consuls are to be transferred to the social security of the industrial economy. The existing capital fund will be dissolved. For the Federal Budget, this means a one-time discharge of € 215 million. It is forecast that from the year 2044 only a burden on the federal budget resulting from a financial gap in the pension fund of architects and engineering consultants will occur. At the same time, this law also amends the rules for registered partners who will be rated as family members in the future. Ladies and gentlemen! I am pleased about this change of law, but above all I am pleased that everyone here in the House agrees to this law. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-12-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00184_d8e22514	We're coming to the 32nd. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. ParlaMint-AT_2012-12-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00184_d8e22544	Mr President. Thank you very much, Mr Sozialminister! Ladies and gentlemen! This government proposal, which we have already discussed in the Committee, is based on a social partners agreement. That is to say, a great deal of thanks to the social partners, the Austrian construction industry, which has the following points that I may briefly explain, which means that there is an agreement. In essence, two laws are affected by this government proposal. On the one hand, the Civilian Vacation and Processing Act: The main content of the changes is that those workers who are paid below-paid – and where this is displayed on the basis of checks carried out by the building workers' holiday and treatment fund – are informed by the holiday fund that they are under-paid so that they can take legal action accordingly. This is a unique and first step that will not only be reimbursed against those entrepreneurs who make under-remuneration, but that those concerned, including workers, will be informed of it. The second is an adaptation of the criminal framework of the construction worker's leave and handling law to the AVRAG. In other words, the penalties for wage and social dumping will be increased accordingly. It is also very nice to have the legal system regarding shift leave. That is to say, all those construction workers, and thus heavy workers who are employed in the shift and who are therefore also more heavily burdened, receive shift leave after eight weeks of employment, i.e. an additional leave from one working day. That is, if someone is working 40 weeks a year as a construction worker in shift work, then an additional five working days are to be paid, i.e. a week of additional leave. The second area addressed here is changes in the building workers' bad weather compensation law. For many decades it has been discussed, today it becomes reality – a very beautiful day for those people who work hard. The term heat will be included in the Bad Weather Act, and it is up to the construction social partners to define the criteria for this on the Board of Directors of the Civilian Vacation and Processing Fund. All those people who work hard, who are exposed to heat, who are exposed to cold, thus have the opportunity to find conditions to be protected in weather conditions under which others no longer visit the free. We're always talking about working longer, that's good, that's good. But with this history and with this enlargement, we also create the framework conditions for the use of workers' protection for those people who are hard to work in the construction industry. I am also grateful for the very open discussion in the Committee on Economic and Monetary Affairs and for the intention of all the parties to this government proposal. However, I am particularly grateful to the Ministry of Social Affairs and all the employees responsible for preparing, implementing and ultimately also to our construction social partner. – Thank you very much.

This is a total of 30 percent of Austrian exports. In comparison, Germany is liable with only 2.8 percent of its exports. Finally, that means that the taxpayer is liable for the banks and exporters. What we demand or encourage is that we think about changing this system. It can't be that the liability is shared at the taxpayer's expense, because at the end of the day, unlike Germany, where the exporters insure themselves against it, the taxpayers have to step in again and again. This is actually just a symptom of the fact that we constantly support the banks, no matter in which areas, too much. Thank you very much.

We count a maximum of € 5 to 7 million. From our point of view, the structural adjustment in the trafiques is also highly condemnable, because I believe that everything must be done to maintain the trafiks. For us in the rural area, the trams now have a lot to do with local services. Very many of the post-partners are located at the Trafiken, and I believe that this is really the last chance that we will receive the post-partners and that at least we will be able to maintain this service in rural areas. In addition, Mr President, Mr Stumpfully has already made it clear that 42 percent of the traffickers are run by disabled people and the disabled. That is why we believe and demand that we reintroduce the 200-piece rule. Especially in the border area, this is one important requirement for existence, and I believe that we can help the Trafiken and the Trafikanten the most. If today one is driving across the border and three people are in the car, then he can get legal Save 300 € to 400 € by taking twelve rods of cigarettes. And the second are the strict controls to really help those who smuggle or who do not comply with these boundaries, to knock on their fingers and thus to help the Trafikanten.
